WHEN ERROR IS THE GRANTING OR REFUSING OF A MOTION 16. WHEN ERROR IS A FINAL DECREE 17. WHEN ERROR IS SUSTAINING OR OVER- RULING A DEMUR R OR POINT OF LAW 18. WHEN ERROR IS NOT WITHIN CLASSI- FICATION 19. VIOLATION OF RULES RELATING TO AS- SIGNMENT OF ERRORS 20. BRIEF OF ARGUMENT, ARRANGEMENT 21. RECORD, OF WHAT CONSISTS 22. RELEVANT DOCKET ENTRIES 23. OMISSION OF EVIDENCE BY AGREE- MENT OR ORDER 24. WHAT MAY BE OMITTED, EVIDENCE, DOCUMENTS 25. DRAFTS, PLANS 

379

26. STATEMENT OF FACTS MAY BE SUB- MITTED FOR ORIGINAL RECORD 27. PRINTING OPINION OF COURT BELOW 28. BRIEFS AND RECORDS SHALL CONFORM WITH RULES 29. BRIEF, APPEALS FROM SUPERIOR COURT 

1. Form, Size and Paging, Printing

Record Separately 

All Briefs and Records must be of white unglazed book paper, nine inches by six inches in size, printed throughout from type at least as large as ten point, and with a margin of not less than one inch. The cover must be so light in color as to permit writing in ink thereon to be easily read, and so firm in texture that the ink will not run. In all cases the 

Record may be printed separately, and must be if it and the Brief together contain more than one hundred pages. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 38. 

2. Front Cover 

On the front cover of the Brief there shall be printed the following matters; 

1. The name of this court and the district in which the appeal is pending; 

2. The term and number of the case in this court; 

3. On appeal from the orphans' court and similar proceeding in other courts, the name of the estate or proceeding and Appeal of (here state the names of appellants); in other appeals the names of the parties as they stood upon the record at the time of the trial or hearing, with the addition of the word "Appellants" after the names of those taking the appeal; 

4. Brief for Appellants or Appellees, or, if the record is in the same book as the Brief, Brief for Appellants and Record or Brief for Appelles and Supplemental Record, as the case may be;

5. Appeal from the judgment, Order or Decree (as the case may be) of the court from which the appeal is taken, with the term and number therein; 

6. The names of counsel, giving the office address of at least one thereof, upon whom it is desired notices shall be served. If the record is printed separately, the cover thereof shall be the same as above provided, except that in place of (4) there shall be printed "Record" or "Supplemental Record," as the fact may be. Unless expressly required, none of the foregoing matters should be repeated in the Brief or Record. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 39. 

3. Index 

The Brief and Record shall each contain a full and complete index, printed on the inside of the front cover, or, when necessary, on the first and immediately succeeding pages, in which event these pages shall not be numbered. The index of the Record, in addition to the matters specified in rule 45, shall include all exhibits, indicating what each is, and the names of witnesses, indicating where the examination, cross-examination and re-examination of each begins. Where the Record is in more than one volume there shall be one continuous paging, regardless of the division into volumes, and but one index, which, however, shall be printed in each volume and shall indicate in which thereof each particular matter will be found. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 40. 

4. Numbering of Pages, Running Headlines 

The pages of the Brief and Record shall be numbered separately in Arabic figures and not in Roman numerals thus; 

1, 2, 3, etc; followed in the Record by a small a, thus, 1a, 2a, 3a, etc.; and every page in each thereof must have a running headline indicating the character of the matter contained on that page; and, when testimony is printed, the name of the witness and whether he is under examination, cross-exami- 25

nation or re-examination. These matters may be inserted in handwriting, if appellant so desires.

Supreme, Superior, Rule 41. (case stated, see Superior 

Rule, 42.) 

5. Brief, Appellant

In all appeals, (except cases stated) the Brief of appellant shall consist of the following matters, headed by the title thereof in distinctive type or in type distinctively displayed, in the following order; 

1. Statement of the questions involved. 

2. History of the case. 

3. When necessary (not in criminal cases) a certificate of the trial judge showing the amount in controversy. 

4. Assignments of error. 

5. Argument for appellant. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 43. 

6. Brief, Appellee

Appellee's brief need contain only his argument, but he may add a counter-history of the case and a counter-statement of the questions involved; unless he does so, or in his brief otherwise challenges those of appellant, it will be assumed he is satisfied with them. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 47. 

7. Statement of Question Involved 

The statement of the questions involved must state the question or questions in the briefest and most general terms, without names, dates, amounts or particulars of any kind. It should not ordinarily exceed fifteen lines, must never exceed one page, must always be printed on the first page of the brief, without any other matter appearing thereon. This rule is to be considered in the highest degree mandatory, admitting of no exception; ordinarily no point will be considered

which is not set forth in the statement of questions involved or suggested thereby. 

Superior, Rule 50; Com. v. Camwell, 91 Pa. Super. 540; 

Com. v. Mellon, 81 Pa. Super. 20; Selected Essays, Moschizisker, 207; Com. v. Snyder, 294 Pa. 555. 

8. History of the Case 

The history of the case must begin with a statement of the form of action, followed by a brief reference to the pleadings, or papers in the nature thereof, upon which the case was tried or heard in the court below, and to any other pleadings or papers which were passed upon by it, if its order or judgment thereon is assigned as error or they are pertinent to the questions raised on the appeal; must include a brief statement of any prior decision of this court, or of the Supreme Court, in the same case or estate, and a reference to the place where it is reported; must give the names of the judges whose rulings are to be reviewed; must contain a closely condensed chronological statement, in narrative form, of all the facts which are necessary to be known in order to determine the points in controversy; and must briefly state the order or decree appealed from; but must not contain any argument or quotations from the testimony. 

Superior, Rule 51; See Supreme Court Rule 51; Com. v. 

Strail, 220 Pa. 483; Com. v. Suspansic, 93 Pa. Super. 111. 

9. Assignment of Errors

Each error relied on must be specified particularly and by itself. If a specification embraces more than one point, or refers to more than one bill of exceptions, or raises more than one distinct question, it may be disregarded. 

Superior Rule 22. Com. v. Diaco, 268 Pa. 305; Com. v. 

Gilleland, 93 Pa. Super. 307; Friedman v. Huber, 92 Pa. Super. 

245; an assignment which has no exception to support it is defective; Com. v. Arcurio, 92 Pa. Super. 404; Com. v. Croce, 89 Pa. Super. 249; Com. v. Norris, 87 Pa. Super. 67; Com. v. 

De Gorge, 89 Pa. Super. 188; Com. v. Mellon, 81 Pa. Super. 20; 

Com. v. Williams, 71 Pa. Super. 311.

'10. Quoting from Record

Whenever matters printed in the record are required to be quoted verbatim in the assignments, it will be sufficient, if such quotations are over a page long, to state briefly the facts in regard thereto and to refer to the pages in the record where they may be found; provided, however, that, whenever this course is pursued, the matter referred to must be enclosed in brackets in the record, so as to show the points of starting and ending, and the number of the assignment must be placed in the margin opposite the first bracket; these insertions, and the above mentioned references to pages in the record, may be made by handwriting, if desired. 

Superior Rule 36; Finkbeiner, v. Philadelphia, R. T. Co. 86 

Pa. Super. 364. · 

11. Assignment of Errors should not be Duplicated

Assignments of error should not be duplicated; one assignment clearly covering a particular point will be suffi cient, though the question involved therein was repeatedly raised, decided and the rulings thereon excepted to in the court below; but in appellant's brief, under the appropriate heads, reference must be made to all the reasons given by the trial tribunal for the rulings complained of, and to the places in the record where they may be found. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule, 23; Com. v. Kovic, 218 Pa. 405; 

Com. v. O'Keefe, 95 Pa. Super. 563; Com. v. Ankoukitis, 83 Pa.

Super. 206, questions not raised in the court below will not be considered on appeal; McCoy v. Hetzl, 84 Pa. Super. 72. 

12. When the Error is to Part of the General Charge

When the error alleged is to a part of the general charge of the court, or to an answer to a point, the assignment must quote verbatim the part of the charge, or the point and its answer, and the exception, unless appellant is excused therefrom by rule thirty-six. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 24; Com. v. Jones, 283 Pa. 564; 

Com. v. Croce, 89 Pa. Super. 249.

13. When Error is to General Effect of Charge 

When the error alleged is to the general effect of the whole charge, including the answer to points, if any, the assignment must quote verbatim the charge and the points and answers, and the exception, unless appellant is excused therefrom by rule thirty-six. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 25; See Act of May 24, 1923, P. L. 

439, 12 P. S. 1197. 

14. When Error is Admission or Rejection of Evidence 

In cases of jury trials, when the error alleged is to the admission or rejection of evidence, or to the striking out or refusing to strike out evidence, the assignment must quote verbatim the question or offer, the objection thereto, the ruling of the court thereon, and the evidence admitted, rejected or stricken out, or which the court refused to strike out, and the exception, unless appellant is excused therefrom by rule thirty-six. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 26, Com. v. Croce 89 Pa. Super. 

249; Hoevelver Stutz Co. v. Cleveland Motor Sales Co. 92 

Pa. Super. 425; Blumberger v. Bernot, 88 Pa. Super. 31; Com. v. Norris, 87 Pa. Super. 66; Finkbeiner v. Phila. R. T. Co., 86 

Pa. Super. 364. 

15. When Error is the Granting or 

Refusing of a Motion

When the error alleged is the granting or refusing of a motion, or the making absolute or discharging of a rule, the assignment must quote verbatim the motion or rule, and the judgment of the court thereon, unless appellant is excused therefrom by rule thirty-six. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 28; Com. v. Bednorciki, 264 Pa. 124. 

16. When Error is a Final Decree

When the error alleged is a final decree, it must be quoted verbatim in the assignment unless appellant is excused therefrom by rule thirty-six. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 29; Com. v. Emery, 273 Pa. 517..

17. When Error is Sustaining or Overruling a Demurrer or Point of Law

When the error alleged is the sustaining or overruling of a demurrer or point of law in the nature thereof raised by the pleadings, the assignment must quote verbatim the grounds thereof and the judgment of the court thereon, unless appellant is excused therefrom by rule thirty-six. 

Superior, Rule 31. 

18. When Error is not within Classification

When the error alleged does not fall within any of the classes above specified, the assignment shall conform to the foregoing requirements as nearly as possible. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 35. 

19. Violation of Rules Relating to 

Assignment of Errors

Assignments of error not in the form prescribed by these rules will be disregarded; but the court reserves to itself the right to notice a plain error duly excepted to, though not assigned or improperly assigned, whenever it deems the justice of the case premptorily requires it. 

Superior, Rule 37; Supreme, Rule 37. 

20. Brief of Argument, Arrangement 

The brief of argument shall be divided into as many parts as there are questions to be argued; shall have at the head of each part, in distinctive type or in type distinctively displayed, the particular point treated therein, followed by such discussion and citation of authorities, as is deemed pertinent; and, under each head, shall refer to the specific assignments of error alleged to raise the question being considered therein. Citation of authorities must set forth the principle for which they are cited. Citations of statutes must be with a reference to the book and page of the pamphlet laws, and also to a standard digest, wherein the statute may be found. Quotations from authorities or statutes must also

set forth the pages from which they are taken. Opinions of this court and of the Supreme Court must be cited from the official reports, if published therein, and in addition may be cited from the Atlantic Reporter; if any such decisions are cited only from legal periodicals, counsel must certify at the end of their argument that they have not been published in the official reports. If reference is made to the pleadings, evidence, charge, opinion, judgment or decree, or any other matter appearing in the record, the printed argument must set forth, in immediate connection therewith, or in a footnote thereto, a reference to the page or pages in the record where the matter referred to appears. When the finding of, or the refusal to find, a fact is argued, the printed brief of argument must contain a synopsis of all the evidence on the point, with a reference to the page or pages of the record where the evidence may be found. 

Superior, Rule 53; Supreme, Rule 53. 

21. Record, of what Consists

The record shall consist of the following matters, headed by the title thereof in distinctive type or in type distinctively displayed, printed in the following order: 

1. The relevant docket entries arranged chronologically. 

2. All pleadings, or papers in the nature thereof (other than those upon which the trial or hearing was had), which were passed upon by the court below (if its action thereon is assigned as error) arranged chronologically. 

3. All opinions of the court below regarding such pleadings or papers (if reported, stating where), and the judgment of the court below thereon, if either assigned as error, or pertinent to the question raised on the appeal. 

4. The pleadings, or papers in the nature thereof, including the bill of particulars, if any, upon which the case was tried or heard. 

5. All opinions (if reported, stating where), and the judgment of the court below on such pleadings or papers, if

either assigned as error, or pertinent to the questions raised on the appeal. 

6. All interlocutory judgments or decrees entered in the case. 

7. So much of the evidence as is necessary to be considered by this court in passing upon the assignments of error. 

8. The charge of the court, including the requests and answers, or the findings and adjudication of the court below, or of the auditor, referee, board, commission, commissioner or master, arranged chronologically. 

9. All motions for judgment non obstante veredicto or in arrest of judgment, or any other motion or order involved in the appeal, and all exceptions filed to the report of the trial judge, the auditor, referee, board, commission, commissioner or master. 

10. All opinions or decisions thereon, if reported, stating where. 

11. The final judgment or decree verbatim. 

Superior, Rule 45; Com. v. Bailey, 92 Pa. Super. 581. 

22. Relevant Docket Entries 

The relevant docket entries of the court below must be printed chronologically in a single column, and shall consist of such parts of the docket entries as are necessary to indicate briefly but clearly the character of the proceedings, the pleadings or papers upon which the case was tried or heard, the trial or hearing, the order, judgment or decree appealed from, all later proceedings appertaining to any of them, and all other matters referred to in the assignments of error or in the argument. The docket entries of the court below, so far as they amplify or do not relate to such matters, must not be printed; but appellee may call attention at the beginning of his counter-history of the case, to any omissions which he may deem important; if he does not do so, it will be assumed he is satisfied with the docket entries as printed by appellant. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 54 See Superior Rules, 45, 59.

23. Omission of Evidence by Agreement or Order 

The appellants and appellees, by writing filed and approved by the lower court, may agree that any part of the evidence appearing in the transcript as certified and filed shall be considered as excluded therefrom upon the review of the case by the supreme court or the superior court; and, if they cannot agree the court below, upon motion of appellants and notice to appellee, may order that any part or portion of the evidence may be omitted by appellant in printing the transcript for the purpose of reviewing such case: 

Provided, however, that appellees may themselves print such evidence, which printing shall be at their own expense, unless it be otherwise ordered by the appellate court; or the appellate court may order any part or all thereof to be printed by appellant, wherever said court shall deem it necessary so to do.1

Anything in the nature of evidence which the court below could reasonably have considered on an appeal to its discretion on a motion for a new trial in a capital case, is properly part of the record for the purpose of review in the supreme court, and should be so certified when requested by the prisoner.2 

1 Act of May 11, 1911, P. L. 279, Sec. 5, 12 P. S. 1200; 

Superior, Rule 55; Com. v. Norris, 87 Pa. Super. 66. 

2 Com. v. Daily, 280 Pa. 59. 

24. What may be Omitted, Evidence, Documents

Evidence which has no relation to or connection with the questions raised by the assignments of error, must not be printed; nor must deeds or long documents, the construction or validity of which are not in issue, but only such parts thereof as are necessary for a proper consideration of the case. 

In order effectually to carry out the foregoing requirement, appellant shall file in the court below, before he commences the printing of the record, a brief statement of the questions he intends to argue on the appeal and of the evidence he does not intend to print, and shall serve copies thereof on appellee, together with a written notice that consent will be

presumed if no objections are made within ten days thereafter, unless the court below, on cause shown, shall grant an extension of time. If objections are made, the court below shall forthwith fix a time for hearing them, and shall then decide the dispute in accordance with, and subject to the provisions of the act of May 11, 1911, P. L. 279. In passing on the materality of evidence, the inquiry shall not be whether the court below deems the evidence substantial, but whether it refers to the particular contention made by the litigant. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 55; Com. v. Loftus, 292 Pa. 395, examination of jurors on voir dire not to be printed where there is no complaint about selection. 

25. Drafts, Plans

When on the trial or hearing in the court below, there is offered in evidence a draft or plan, which would be of assistance to this court to enable it to understand readily the dispute between the parties, a copy thereof must be attached to appellant's record or handed up therewith, folded of the same size as the record. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 44. 

26. Statement of Facts may be Submitted for Original Record

When the questions presented by an appeal can be determined by this court without an examination of all the pleadings and evidence, the parties in interest may prepare and sign a statement of the case showing how the questions arose and were decided in the court below, and setting forth only so many of the facts alleged and proved, or sought to be proved, as are essential to a decision of the questions by the court. Such statements shall be filed in the court below and a copy thereof shall be served upon the judge who tried or decided the case, and it shall be treated as superseding, for the purposes of the appeal, all other parts of the record except the order, judgment or decree from which the appeal is taken, and, together therewith, shall be copied and certified to this court as the record on appeal. So, also, the parties may

substitute an agreed statement of facts for all or any part of the evidence and print it in lieu thereof at the place where otherwise the evidence would have been printed. 

Supreme, Superior, Rule 56. 

27. Printing Opinion of Court Below 

Whenever an opinion of the court below, required to be printed under these rules, refers to and relies upon some other published opinion, the place of publication of which is not stated, appellant's brief must set forth the place of publication thereof and of any dissenting opinion in the cited case; if not published, either party may print a copy thereof, giving the names of the judge rendering them and the dates of their filing. 

Superior, Rule 49. 

28. Briefs and Records shall Conform with Rules 

Briefs and records shall conform in all material respects with the requirements of these rules, as nearly as the circumstances of the particular case will admit, otherwise they may be suppressed, and, if the defects are in appellant's Brief or Record and are substantial, the appeal may be quashed. 

Superior, 57, Supreme, Rule 57. 

29. Brief, Appeals from Superior Court 

On appeals from the superior court, appellants may prepare new briefs according to the Supreme Court rules, setting forth also the petition for and order following the appeal, and the opinion and dissenting opinions, if any, in that court (if reported, stating where) or may utilize the briefs used therein, (changing the cover, however), and adding thereto the assignment of errors in the court. The petition for an order allowing the appeal, the opinion and dis-

senting opinion, if any, of that court (if reported, stating where), and such additional argument as may be desired. 

Appellee may also prepare a new brief, or may utilize the one used in the superior court, with such additional argument as may be desired. 

Supreme, Rule 48.

My Commission Expires INSTRUCTIONS to Justice of the Peace 

1. You are not permitted to take bail in the following cases, viz: Murder, manslaughter, arson, rape, felonious rape, mayhem, sodomy, buggery, robbery, burglary. 

2. When the defendant is held, take bail to the NEXT term of COURT. 

3. The law requires you to take and return the names, residences and occupations, if any, of the defendant, bail and witnesses. Failure to do so is a misdemeanor, punishable by a fine of $300. By residence is meant the post office address.

4. The law requires you to return all cases of felony within 

FIVE DAYS after the hearing; failure to do so is a misdemeanor punishable by a fine not exceeding $300. In all cases the return should be made at once. 

5. See that the Justification of Surety is properly filled up and that all of the information required as to the identity of the surety, condition of his property, etc., is given clearly and correctly and verified by affidavit. 

6. Bail, in all cases, should be commensurate with the nature and seriousness of the offense charged, and in nonsupport and surety of the peace cases the bail should be made returnable forthwith, instead of to the next term of court. 

7. In surety of the peace cases you should follow the procedure as prescribed by Act of Assembly of March 18, 1909, P. L. 42, and if you bind the defendant over to the Court of 

Quarter Sessions, the transcript of your docket herewith should show that you have complied with said Act. 

8. In the event of disregard of any of the foregoing requirements in addition to incurring the penalty provided by law, the committing magistrate may be liable to forfeit all costs in the case.

No. 27

Points for Charge

The Commonwealth of Pennsylvania 

POINTS FOR CHARGE 

The defendant respectfully requests the learned trial judge to charge the jury as follows: VS. 

J. N. No. County, Sess., 19 

1. If after consideration of all the evidence in the case, a reasonable doubt exists in the minds of the jurors as to the innocence or guilt of the defendant, the defendant is entitled to its benefit and should be acquitted. Answer: 

2. A reasonable doubt may exist in the mind of a juror without his being able to give a reason for the doubt. Answer: 

3. Jurors have the right to determine the law and the facts under the direction of the court. They have the right to determine, by their verdict, the result of applying the law, as stated by the court, to the facts as they find them. Answer: 28

4. Evidence of good reputation, produced in behalf of the defendant, is substantial evidence, to be weighed and considered by the jury in connection with the other evidence in the case.

Good character is of importance in this, that it may of itself, in spite of all evidence to the contrary, raise a reasonable doubt in the minds of the jury, and so produce an acquittal.

Answer: 

5. Circumstantial evidence must be incompatible with defendant's innocence upon any rational theory, and incapable of explanation upon any other reasonable hypothesis than that of guilt, otherwise the defendant should be acquitted.

Answer: 

6. Under the law and the evidence your verdict should be "Not Guilty."

Answer: 

Attorney for Defendant.

5. That in said sentence and judgment and prior proceedings there are manifest errors which are specified in detail in the assignment of errors filed herewith. 

6. The amount of bail which the defendant was under in the Court below was dollars.

7. A copy of the indictment is attached hereto and made a part hereof. 

8. An appeal was taken on the 19.

Therefore, your petitioner prays that an order may be granted making said appeal a supersedeas to stay the sentence imposed, until the said appeal can be determined and disposed of, upon such conditions and terms as your honorable court may see fit to impose. (Affidavit) day of Defendant

Assignment of Errors

1. The Court erred in admitting in evidence over the objection and exception of the defendant, Commonwealth

3. The Court erred in charging the jury as follows: 

Attorney for Defendant

No. 43 

Supersedeas Order 

And now, to wit, it is ordered that the appeal in this case shall operate as a supersedeas upon the defendant's giving bail with one or more sureties to be approved by the Court of Quarter Sessions of County or a judge thereof, conditioned that he will prosecute his appeal with effect and will abide by and comply with all orders and the judgment of the Superior Court in the cause: amount of bail fixed at dollars; due notice of the time and place of entering bail to be given to the District Attorney. 

By the Court Judge

Whereas, the above-named defendant, A. In the Court of vs. 

M. M.

PETITION The Commonwealth of Pennsylvania of Pennsylvania No. County,

Sess., 19____. 

Petition for Rule to Take Testimony of Nonresident witness. (Affidavit)

To the Honorable the Judges of Said Court: 

The petition of M. M. respectfully

That he is the defendant in the above-entitled action. 

That A. B. who resides at ness on behalf of defendant in the above-styled action. is a material wit- 

That the said A. B. has not and cannot be subpoenaed, or his attendance otherwise procured. 

Wherefore, he prays the court to grant a rule to take the testimony of the said A. B. to be used on behalf of the defendant at the trial of the above-entitled case. 

And he will ever pray, etc.

No. 47 Rule 

19____, upon motion of 

And now attorney for defendant, it is ordered that the deposition of who resides at , may be taken by the defendant before any person authorized by the state of to take depositions. Said deposition to be taken days, upon giving within days' notice to the district attorney of the time at which, and place where, the deposition shall be taken. •9 Judge

Note: The said rule shall be entered in the office of the clerk of the court, and a copy thereof, with notice of the time and place and names of the witness or witnesses, signed by the party or his attorney, shall be served upon the district attorney, which notice shall be in accordance with the order of court.

Keeper of 

To the Honorable 

Common Pleas of county jail No. Witnesses (Affidavit) County,

The petition of C. M. respectfully represent: That he is unjustly and unlawfully detained and imprisoned in the custody of the keeper of the county jail. 

Judge of the Court of County:

And he will ever pray, etc.
